## Purpose

This file is a **working scratchpad for new, not-yet-merged documentation**. Treat it as the current source of truth **only for the specific items it explicitly covers**. For everything else, PF-Canon (PF01, PF02, PF04, PF05, PF09, PF12, PF14, PF19, PF20, etc.) remains the single home.

---

## **Precedence, versioning, and scope**

### **1\. PF10 authority**

PF10 IS CANONICAL. For any topic explicitly addressed by an applicable, active PF10 addendum, that addendum is the current authoritative source of truth and supersedes conflicting permanent PF canon until the guidance is formally reviewed and drained into the relevant permanent PF document.

No competing canon may be used against an applicable PF10 addendum. Agents must not prefer, merge, reinterpret, or reconcile conflicting permanent-canon language while the PF10 guidance remains active.

PF10 authority is limited to the scope actually addressed by its addenda. The presence of a topic elsewhere in a PF10 document does not make PF10 authoritative over related topics that no addendum addresses.

### **2\. Scope within an individual PF10 document**

An individual PF10 document is a container for multiple numbered addenda. It must not be treated as one undifferentiated rule or as blanket authority over every topic associated with the current epic.

Each addendum is an independent unit of authority with its own scope. That scope is established by the addendum’s heading and substantive content, including the decisions, requirements, exceptions, artifacts, behaviors, processes, or canon topics it explicitly addresses.

Within one PF10 document:

1. Every active, non-superseded addendum remains authoritative for its own stated scope.  
2. A higher-numbered addendum does not automatically invalidate every lower-numbered addendum in the document.  
3. Unrelated addenda remain independently authoritative and must be applied together when more than one is relevant to the current task.  
4. An addendum must not be applied outside the scope established by its actual content.  
5. The entire document must be searched for relevant addenda. Agents must not consult only the first, last, or highest-numbered addendum.  
6. Document-level relevance does not establish addendum-level relevance. An agent must identify which specific addendum or addenda govern the current question.

For example, if addendum `2.12` governs evidence indexing and addendum `2.18` governs compatibility routing, both remain authoritative within their separate scopes. The existence of `2.18` does not supersede `2.12` merely because `2.18` has a higher number.

### **3\. Single-document and multi-document PF10 versions**

An active PF10 base version may consist of either:

1. One unlettered document, such as `10.x.x`; or  
2. One multi-document lettered set, such as `10.x.xa`, `10.x.xb`, `10.x.xc`, and so forth.

A lettered set may be used when an epic’s build notes exceed the practical capacity of one document and drainage before the end of the epic is not practical.

All documents sharing the same base version and carrying sequential letter suffixes belong to the same PF10 version set. For example:

* `10.x.xa`  
* `10.x.xb`  
* `10.x.xc`

Together, these documents constitute the complete active `10.x.x` PF10 set.

The letter suffix identifies a physical document within the set. It does not create a newer base version, determine topical scope, or give one document greater authority than another.

All documents in the active lettered set are equally authoritative containers of PF10 addenda. A later lettered document extends the set. It does not replace, obsolete, drain, or supersede an earlier lettered document merely because it has a later suffix.

For example, `10.x.xb` does not replace `10.x.xa`. Active, non-superseded addenda in both documents remain authoritative for their respective scopes.

### **4\. Continuous addendum sequence**

A lettered PF10 set must preserve one continuous addendum-number sequence across all documents in the set.

Each subsequent lettered document must begin with the addendum number immediately following the final addendum in the preceding document.

Example:

* `10.x.xa` contains addenda through `2.28`.  
* `10.x.xb` begins with addendum `2.29`.  
* If `10.x.xb` ends with addendum `2.41`, `10.x.xc` begins with addendum `2.42`.

The numbered addendum sequence establishes the order of PF10 guidance. The document-letter sequence identifies where that guidance is stored.

### **5\. Individual-addendum supersession**

Supersession operates at the individual-addendum level, not automatically at the document level.

This rule applies:

* Between addenda in the same individual PF10 document.  
* Between addenda located in different documents within the same lettered PF10 set.

Each addendum remains authoritative for its stated scope unless a higher-numbered addendum revises, replaces, narrows, expands, or otherwise supersedes that scope.

When two or more addenda address the same or overlapping scope:

1. The highest-numbered applicable addendum is the sole authority for the overlapping scope.  
2. Conflicting or replaced language in every lower-numbered addendum is superseded and must not be applied in parallel.  
3. A lower-numbered addendum remains authoritative for any distinct scope not superseded by the higher-numbered addendum.  
4. If the higher-numbered addendum explicitly supersedes the earlier addendum in full, no portion of the earlier addendum remains authoritative.  
5. Supersession applies regardless of whether the addenda appear in the same document or in different documents within the active lettered set.

Example within one document:

* Addendum `2.17` establishes a rule.  
* Addendum `2.22` in the same document revises that rule.  
* Addendum `2.22` controls the revised scope.  
* Addendum `2.17` remains authoritative only for any distinct scope that `2.22` does not supersede.

Example across a lettered set:

* Addendum `2.17` appears in `10.x.xa`.  
* Addendum `2.34` appears in `10.x.xb` and revises the same rule.  
* Addendum `2.34` controls the overlapping scope.  
* Addendum `2.17` must not be used as competing or parallel authority for that scope.  
* Any distinct portion of addendum `2.17` not superseded by `2.34` remains authoritative.

A later document letter alone supersedes nothing. Addendum number and addendum scope determine supersession.

### **6\. Current-version rule**

Only the latest active PF10 base version matters. That version may be represented by one unlettered document or by a complete lettered set.

Older base versions are fully drained, obsolete, or both. Once a newer base version exists, agents must not read, reuse, compare, reconcile, or carry forward content from an older base version.

This rule does not apply between documents belonging to the same active lettered set. If `10.x.xa`, `10.x.xb`, and `10.x.xc` form the active version, all three remain part of the authoritative PF10 source set.

An earlier lettered document remains authoritative for every applicable addendum it contains that has not been superseded by a higher-numbered addendum. It must not be discarded merely because a later lettered document exists.

### **7\. Live-content rule**

The active PF10 document or complete lettered set contains the current live guidance that has not yet been drained into its permanent PF home.

Drained guidance is removed when PF10 is formally revised.

If a lower-numbered addendum remains physically present after a higher-numbered addendum supersedes some or all of its scope, the superseded language is no longer live authority. Physical presence does not defeat individual-addendum supersession.

All unaffected, non-superseded addenda within the same document or lettered set remain live and authoritative for their respective scopes.

### **8\. Silence rule**

PF10 governs only through applicable, active, non-superseded addenda.

PF10 is silent when no addendum in the complete active PF10 document or lettered set addresses the current topic.

When PF10 is silent, authority returns to the relevant permanent PF-Canon document.

PF10 silence must be determined only after checking:

1. Every potentially relevant addendum within each applicable individual document.  
2. Every document belonging to the complete active lettered set, when a lettered set exists.  
3. Any higher-numbered addendum that may have superseded earlier guidance.

### **9\. Operational rule for agents**

Agents must apply PF10 in this order:

1. Identify the latest active PF10 base version.  
2. Determine whether it consists of one unlettered document or a multi-document lettered set.  
3. If it is a lettered set, identify all documents sharing that base version, such as `10.x.xa`, `10.x.xb`, and `10.x.xc`.  
4. Treat each document as a container of separately scoped numbered addenda.  
5. Search every applicable document for all addenda relevant to the current topic.  
6. Determine the actual scope of each relevant addendum from its heading and substantive content.  
7. Compare the scopes of all relevant addenda, including addenda within the same document.  
8. Where addendum scopes overlap, apply only the highest-numbered applicable addendum to the overlapping scope.  
9. Continue applying lower-numbered addenda to any distinct scope not superseded by a higher-numbered addendum.  
10. Do not infer document-level supersession from a later letter suffix.  
11. Ignore all older PF10 base versions.  
12. Fall back to permanent PF canon only when the complete active PF10 document or lettered set contains no applicable, active, non-superseded guidance for the topic.

## Cross-references

Inside this file, all references to PF documents MUST be **titles-only** (for example “HDE-Phased Epics”, “Glow QA Guide”), never file names or version numbers in the body text.

When editing or extending this file, AI Agent sessions must:

* Not restate PF content here.  
    
* Link by **document title and section only**.

# **2\) Numbered Addenda**

## **2.1 GCFPE model and reasoning-level guidance is the TypeSafe reading; no AI recommendation is given**

Timestamp: 101126 20:47 (UTC) Details: Establishes that, in the Glow Change Flow Prompt Ecosystem, the model and reasoning-level guidance for a downstream session is the reading of the installed TypeSafe scoring skill, and that no agent gives its own model, reasoning-level or effort recommendation. Determines the question addendum 2.38 of the previous PF10 version left open for Product Owner determination.

### **Source and authority**

This addendum records the Product Owner's directions of 2026-10-11:

* "My whole point with this was to add typesafe scoring system to the GCFPE. random model advice is useless"  
* "I absolutely do not want a claude recommendation. No research I have found has demonstrated that an AI model is able to effectively choose model scoring ... I have found the typesafe scoring to deliver highly reliable results."

Where this addendum conflicts with earlier PF-Canon, this addendum governs for the scope below.

### **Rule**

1. Scope. Every GCFPE downstream-session prompt and handoff, and every management or facilitation relay that hands GCFPE work to a session.  
2. The reading. The model and reasoning-level guidance for that session is the TypeSafe reading produced by the installed TypeSafe scoring skill, reported as the skill prints it: its cell, every probability and its flags. The Product Owner picks the model and setting, and the pick is recorded with the reading.  
3. No AI recommendation. No agent gives its own recommendation, assessment or ranking of model, reasoning level or effort, beside the reading or in place of it.  
4. No reading. A reading that cannot be obtained is reported as not read, with the reason. No substitute recommendation is given.  
5. Provider neutrality. HDE Governance §9.1.3's rule that no governance rule requires a specific AI provider, product, model or effort level does not apply to the TypeSafe scoring skill within this scope. It continues to apply to the model and surface that perform the work: the reading informs the Product Owner's pick and requires no model.

### **Superseded passages and drain targets**

| Document and section | Superseded scope |
| :---- | :---- |
| HDE Governance §9.1.3, the sentence beginning "Every downstream-session prompt must receive task-specific human advice" | For the GCFPE, advice on model and reasoning level is replaced by rules 2 to 4\. Advice on execution surface is unchanged. |
| HDE Governance §9.1.6 | The Managing Prompt Engineer's "model guidance" for the GCFPE is the reading in rule 2\. |

### **Status, effects and nonclaims**

* No epic, task or PF09 row changes status by this addendum.  
* The GCFPE prompt and skill changes that implement it are unperformed.  
* This addendum does not extend to other ecosystems, select any model, or make a reading an approval or gate.

## 2.2 HDE-CRD-0002 WU-PR-01: two HDAPI v2 contract-inventory evidence tools join the CI-coverage work unit for bounded corrections; the legacy chart-loader test is retired with its defect routed to the Product Owner

Timestamp: 101226 23:07 (UTC) Details: Records the whole-change Implementation Agent's approval, through Review Bounded Work-Unit Rescope, of one bounded implementation rescope of work unit WU-PR-01 (CI coverage of every test file; requirements S03, S04, S05) of the approved HDE-CRD-0002 Implementation Plan v1.1. Origin: the WU-PR-01 rescope request v1.0 (items RP-1 and RP-2) from the dedicated PR engineering session during pre-Proceed planning. Evidence: the review's own reproduction of both findings at main 6c664dd9 and at cf9198df, and its reading of the tools, the adapter, the classifier and the loader.

### Decision and approval status

The continuing whole-change Implementation Agent for HDE-CRD-0002 decided APPROVE on the WU-PR-01 rescope request v1.0, as one state for the one request. The decision record is docs/ephemeral/HDE-CRD-0002-WU-PR-01-rescope-review-v1.0.md. The approval is a bounded implementation rescope within the approved Specification's intent; it is not a change to the Epic-level commitment, to any acceptance criterion, protected boundary, other work unit, accepted dependency or Product Owner matter. The immutable base is not rewritten. This addendum is the one overlay the approval creates; the effective baseline for WU-PR-01 is the approved base plus this addendum.

### Base

HDE-CRD-0002 Specification v1.1, requirement S03 ("Bring every test file into a CI lane or retire it with a recorded reason"; criterion "No test file runs in no CI lane; each retirement has a recorded reason; recorded failures and collection errors are fixed or retired with their files"; constraint "S03 may expose real defects that enlarge the work"). Implementation Plan v1.1, work unit WU-PR-01 (deliverables, acceptance (1) to (5), risk R-1). WU-PR-01 PR instruction v1.0 §6 (bounded files: fixes stay inside test code and test-local fixtures), §7 (no change to any tools/\*\* or scripts/\*\* file), §9.2 (fix-versus-retire criterion), §9.8 (a real product defect is not fixed by changing a test's expectation; a needed non-test change is routed) and §14 (rescope behavior). None of these is changed.

### Approved delta (RP-1)

WU-PR-01's bounded files are extended by exactly two items, and the instruction's tools/\*\* exclusion is relaxed for exactly the changes stated here:

1. tools/evidence/generate\_hdapi\_v2\_contract\_inventory.py: only the data of ADAPTER\_BOUNDARY\_PUBLIC\_ROUTE\_BASELINE\_RECORDS changes. It is rebuilt from the public route records the three app factories register at the PR head and reviewed against HDE CLI/API/Vendor Reference §5.1.0 and §5.4 before commit. It must contain reader\_post, reader\_method\_not\_allowed and reader\_post\_not\_allowed at /api and must not contain the removed reader\_v1\_post. The generator's outputs, schema, evidence family, refusal codes, flags and write paths do not change; it gains no \--check mode or other feature; it is not run in write mode against the repository.  
2. tools/evidence/hdapi\_v2\_boundary\_analyzer.py: the change is confined to presenter-provenance resolution in \_adapter\_routes\_without\_presenter and any private helper it needs inside the analyzer. Provenance through a helper is accepted only when the view calls a module-level helper of its own module with an argument whose bound value, or whose parameter default, is a sanctioned presenter callable (the production Reader's get\_reader\_api\_bp → \_production\_reader\_response(emit\_fn) shape, whose default is emit\_reader\_public\_bytes). Bypass detection is not weakened: a parameter whose default is not a sanctioned presenter callable, a helper that emits through any other path, a parameter with neither a sanctioned default nor a sanctioned bound value, and every case reported today stay reported.

With them, inside files WU-PR-01 already owns:

* ci/checks/classify\_ci\_changes.py: \_EVIDENCE\_GENERATOR\_TEST\_OWNERS\["tools/evidence/generate\_hdapi\_v2\_contract\_inventory.py"\] \= ("tests/evidence/test\_hdapi\_v2\_contract\_inventory.py",); tools/evidence/hdapi\_v2\_boundary\_analyzer.py leaves \_EVIDENCE\_HELPERS\_REQUIRING\_OWNER and enters \_EVIDENCE\_HELPER\_TEST\_OWNERS with owners tests/evidence/test\_hdapi\_v2\_contract\_inventory.py and \_EVIDENCE\_HELPER\_OWNERSHIP\_TEST. These two source keys are the only exception to the detailed plan's rule that no new source key is created.  
* tests/evidence/test\_hdapi\_v2\_contract\_inventory.py: the 103 tmp\_boundary\_test fixture sites move to tmp\_path; one positive analyzer case (the shape above is accepted) and one negative case (a helper with a non-presenter default is reported and fails the analyzer verdict) are added; the file is registered under the detailed plan's registration rule.  
* tests/evidence/test\_rails\_ci\_workflow\_integration.py: every pin over the owner registries stays true.

### Binding requirements

* The file passes 298 of 298 plus the two new cases with ruby on PATH, in the detached-worktree full-validation run, with a clean tree and a clean mtime check. No test is skipped, deselected, xfail-marked or quarantined.  
* changed\_test\_targets returns the test for each tool (plus the ownership test for the analyzer).  
* The lane that runs the file has ruby available or the run fails; it never skips. The detailed plan records ruby as a visible dependency and names where the lane obtains it.  
* No governed artifact changes: the diff from base to head over artifacts/vendor/hdapi\_v2/, docs/evidence/, artifacts/evidence\_index.jsonl and its checksum, and artifacts/architecture/ is empty; the frozen HDE-EPIC033 to HDE-EPIC035 captures are unchanged; the architecture snapshot test stays green. Neither tool gains a new import symbol, because the architecture snapshot records both by import-symbol keys; a needed new import is a new finding to the Implementation Agent, never a regeneration.  
* If the analyzer change cannot be made without weakening bypass detection, that is a new finding to the Implementation Agent through the rescope lane; it is not resolved by changing a test.  
* The detailed plan states the mechanism by which a route change in adapter/factory.py or adapter/http\_reader.py re-runs this test, so that the baseline stays current when WU-PR-03 changes those routes.  
* WU-PR-01's acceptance (1) to (5), its interface contracts C-1 to C-6, its evidence obligations, its nonclaims and its PR-30/PR-35 split are unchanged.

### RP-2: in scope under the base; no delta

tests/loader/test\_chart\_loader\_uses\_provider\_and\_is\_safe\_mode\_default.py is retired under the base's own authority (S03; instruction §9.2; the detailed plan's retirement class for a legacy signature no current entrypoint reaches). Its ledger row records the reason: the file exposes a product defect in the legacy signature of engine/charts/loader.py (\_call\_legacy calls get\_chart on a FixturesProvider that has none, since 6c6f4b2a, 2025-09-28); only tests call it; the loader is not a release member and is not reached at runtime; the defect is routed to the Product Owner as the decision owner for Task HDE-SEPA006 (HDE Build Checklist — Separation; HDE Architecture §1.1 "Non-BodyGraph chart loader classification"); it is not fixed in HDE-CRD-0002. Replacing coverage for its passing case: tests/provider/test\_vendor\_and\_prod\_guard\_min.py::test\_vendor\_refuses\_in\_safe\_mode and tests/provider/test\_vendor\_call\_rails\_and\_config.py::test\_vendor\_refused\_when\_safe\_mode\_on, both retained and passing at the PR head. engine/charts/loader.py, engine/provider/fixtures.py and every other non-test file stay unchanged; the tracked artifacts/logs/loader\_call.jsonl is not edited, truncated or regenerated. Neither the ledger nor the PR result record represents the defect as fixed, deferred to a unit of HDE-CRD-0002, or resolved.

### Scope, exclusions and nonclaims

* No other tools/\*\* or scripts/\*\* file changes; ci/jobs/\*.yml and ci/checks/run\_rails\_job\_definitions.py are unchanged.  
* No release member changes; catalog/manifest.json, engine/config/registry\_loader.py and the admission constants are unchanged; no manifest refresh.  
* No public route, public payload, public flag, transport or runtime behavior changes; the production Reader continues to emit only through the presenter.  
* No live vendor, production or database call; closed rails throughout.  
* No QA artifact, QA verdict, acceptance, PF09 or PF30 status movement, deployment, canon edit, Product Owner Proceed, merge authority or epic/CRD closure follows from this addendum.  
* Retiring the contract-inventory test together with both tools as historical was not decided; it remains a separate product decision the Product Owner may raise.  
* A repair of engine/charts/loader.py was not approved.

### Affected surfaces

* Requirements. S03 acceptance (1) and (2) become reachable; S04, S05 and acceptance (3) to (5) are unaffected; S03 remains classified as not production-affecting.  
* Dependencies. None upstream. The coverage definition contracted to WU-PR-06 is unchanged; WU-PR-06's sweep environment must provide ruby for this file.  
* Tests. Two cases added; one file retired. The detailed plan's dispositions become 42 retained, 27 retained-and-fixed and 66 retired, restated by plan v1.1 from its own table.  
* Ops. None.  
* Documentation. The undeclared ruby dependency of the generator (the detailed plan's observation OBS-12) is a documentation delta candidate for the Product Owner.  
* Downstream work. WU-PR-03 later changes the adapter routes and must keep the generator's baseline current; the Implementation Agent carries that into the WU-PR-03 PR instruction. The WU-PR-06 instruction carries the ruby requirement of the sweep environment.

### Unresolved obligations and owners

## 2.3 Codex security review is deprecated: it is no longer a step of the HDE pull-request review chain

Timestamp: 101326 02:55 (UTC). Details: records the Product Owner's deprecation of the automated Codex security review, which is no longer available. Origin: the HDE-CRD-0002 WU-PR-01 PR-35 session for pull request [amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2\#627](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2/pull/627). Evidence: the Codex review summary of [\#627](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2/pull/627) and the precedent of [\#501](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2/issues/501) (below).

### Source and authority

This addendum records the Product Owner's directions of 2026-10-13, given in the HDE-CRD-0002 WU-PR-01 PR-35 session:

* "This feature (security review) is no longer available. We will need to deprecate it. You may create a PF10 build notes addendum to specify its deprecation."  
* Before that direction, the Product Owner's decision for [\#627](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2/pull/627) was "Waive it for [\#627](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2/pull/627)", given in answer to the question of how PR-35 should treat the security review that the HDE-CRD-0002 Implementation Plan names in its reviewer chain and that never ran on [\#627](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2/pull/627).

Where this addendum conflicts with earlier PF-Canon or with an in-flight change document, this addendum governs for the scope below.

### Rule

1. Scope. The automated Codex security review of a pull request: the "Security Review" row of the Codex review summary, produced when a pull request opens or requested by an @codex security review comment. It is covered wherever HDE canon, an in-flight change document, a GCFPE prompt or a skill names it as part of a pull request's review chain.  
2. Deprecation. The Codex security review is not part of the HDE pull-request review chain. It is not obtained or waited on, and its absence is not treated as a review gap, finding, blocker or waiver need, by any of: a pull request, a work unit, a merge-readiness decision, a lineage review, a QA readiness decision or a closure decision. A MERGE\_PENDING result does not depend on it.  
3. Unchanged. The Codex code review remains part of the pull-request review chain and is read and resolved as before. Every other security obligation is unchanged, including:  
   * security constraints in permanent canon;  
   * security constraints in approved Specifications and Implementation Plans;  
   * security constraints in the repository's agent instructions;  
   * security deliverables of a work unit, such as the recorded code security review that HDE-CRD-0002 requirement S19 assigns to WU-PR-05, a recorded review that adds no review tooling.  
4. No substitute. This addendum creates no replacement review tool, review step, approval or gate.  
5. Historical records. Records of security reviews that ran or were missed on earlier pull requests keep their wording and remain historical statements, for example the HDE Phased Epics §2.26.7 RA-09 security-coverage limitation and the Reality Audits finding RA-09.

### Passages read under this rule

These passages are not rewritten. From the turn this addendum is created, each is read without the Codex security review:

| Location | Passage | Reading under this addendum |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| HDE-CRD-0002 Implementation Plan v1.1, §7 "Ordered work units", common to every PR unit (docs/ephemeral/HDE-CRD-0002-implementation-plan-v1.1.md) | "reviewer chain \= Codex code review and security review on the PR" | reviewer chain \= Codex code review on the PR, PR-35 for review resolution, PR-40 lineage review |
| HDE-CRD-0002 WU-PR-01 PR instruction v1.0, §12 (docs/ephemeral/HDE-CRD-0002-WU-PR-01-pr-instruction-v1.0.md) | PR-35 "retrieves and resolves review findings (Codex code review and security review on the PR)" | PR-35 retrieves and resolves the Codex code review findings |
| HDE-CRD-0002 WU-PR-01 detailed PR plan v1.1, §19 (docs/ephemeral/HDE-CRD-0002-WU-PR-01-pr-implementation-plan-v1.1.md) | "(Codex code review and security review)" | the Codex code review |
| Later HDE-CRD-0002 PR instructions and detailed plans issued under Implementation Plan v1.1 | the same reviewer chain | the same reading |
| HDE-CRD-0002 Specification v1.1, routed decision RD-1 (docs/ephemeral/HDE-CRD-0002-specification-v1.1-approved.md) | the residual of item 6(a), "security-review coverage of pushes after the first", routed to GCFPE-MGMT-10 and the Product Owner | the residual concerned the deprecated review, and no coverage of that review remains to be provided |
| GCFPE prompts that direct reading a pull request's security reviews (PR-35 — Resolve PR Reviews and Reach Merge Readiness) and the PR development skill that names "Codex code and security reviews" | reading "applicable" security reviews | no Codex security review is applicable |

Permanent PF canon names no Codex security review. The HDE Governance §9.1.1 authority table entry for PR implementation, "Normal substantive code/security/review and evidence chain", is not reworded by this addendum.

### Evidence

* On [\#627](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2/pull/627) the Codex review summary showed only a "Code Review" row through six heads, from 2026-10-13T00:55Z to 02:47Z. No "Security Review" row appeared.  
* One @codex security review comment on [\#627](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2/pull/627) (comment 6135990857, 01:19:59Z) was answered by a Code Review run with trigger "Manual request", and still no "Security Review" row appeared.  
* On [\#501](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2/issues/501) the "Security Review" row came only from "PR opened", and a later @codex security review comment was answered by a code-review comment.

### Status, effects and nonclaims

* No epic, CRD, task, PF09 or PF30 row changes status by this addendum.  
* The wording changes to GCFPE prompts and skills that implement this addendum are unperformed.  
* This addendum does not change the Codex code review or any security requirement of canon or of a change. It does not waive a security finding, and it establishes no QA, acceptance, merge or closure.  
* For [\#627](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2/pull/627), the Product Owner's earlier waiver and this deprecation agree: [\#627](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2/pull/627) has no Codex security review to obtain.

### Unresolved obligations and owners

* Whether the HDE pull-request review chain needs another source of security review is not decided by this addendum. That includes the wording of HDE Governance §9.1.1's chain entry. Owner: the Product Owner.  
* The wording of the GCFPE prompts and skills that name the Codex security review. Owner: GCFPE-MGMT-10 and the Product Owner.

## 2.6 HDE-CRD-0002 WU-PR-03: the dev Reader start helper keeps propagating APP\_ENV exactly, as canon requires, and the engine-core purity report leaves the current-release label roster

Timestamp: 101426 02:40 (UTC) Details: Records the whole-change Implementation Agent's approval, through Review Bounded Work-Unit Rescope, of one bounded implementation rescope of work unit WU-PR-03 (public, dev and CLI surfaces; requirements S02, S07, S09, S10, S15, S18 and the same-version release refresh) of the approved HDE-CRD-0002 Implementation Plan v1.1.

* Origin: the WU-PR-03 rescope request v1.0 (items RP-1, RP-3 and RP-2) from the dedicated PR engineering session during pre-Proceed planning.  
* Evidence: the review's reading of the canon passages on the dev Reader start helper, and its own reproduction at main 852370c of the release refresh, the stale label and its correction.

### Decision and approval status

The continuing whole-change Implementation Agent for HDE-CRD-0002 decided APPROVE on the WU-PR-03 rescope request v1.0, as one state for the one request. The decision record is docs/ephemeral/HDE-CRD-0002-WU-PR-03-rescope-review-v1.0.md.  
The approval is a bounded implementation rescope within the approved Specification's intent. It does not change the Epic-level commitment, any Specification acceptance criterion, any protected boundary, any other work unit, any accepted dependency or any Product Owner matter. The immutable base is not rewritten.  
This addendum is the one overlay the approval creates. The effective baseline for WU-PR-03 is the approved base plus addenda 2.2, 2.3 and 2.5 plus this addendum.  
The canon-conflict register entry CR-6 is APPROVED with alternative (a): canon governs.

### Base

The base is not changed. Its relevant clauses are:

* Specification v1.1:  
  * requirement S10 ("The dev route is served only when APP\_ENV is explicitly dev, test or local; unset or any other value refuses"), narrowed by the approved Plan to explicit dev;  
  * the constraint "S10 must not break dev, test or local use of /reader";  
  * requirement S09;  
  * requirement S12 ("Current-release captures carry current-release labels").  
* Implementation Audit v1.0, engineering choice EC-4: "scripts/dev\_start\_reader.sh defaults APP\_ENV=dev when unset; docs/server/reader\_v1.md states it".  
* Implementation Plan v1.1:  
  * interface IF-7 (release labels on rows for captures the current release regenerates);  
  * interface IF-10, whose last clause is "scripts/dev\_start\_reader.sh defaults APP\_ENV=dev";  
  * work unit WU-PR-03 "Deliverables" and acceptance (2), whose last clause is "scripts/dev\_start\_reader.sh without APP\_ENV starts a serving runner".  
* Plan review v1.0, redline R1: the explicit-dev gate, applied in review v1.1. R1 kept EC-4's default.  
* WU-PR-03 PR instruction v1.0:  
  * §4, §5.5, §6, §8 contract E-6, §9 items 5 and 6, and §10 carry the default;  
  * §5.7 (c) states that a roster capture left byte-stable across a release\_id change keeps its earlier label and "is a finding to the IA … not a roster edit";  
  * §6 keeps tools/evidence/update\_evidence\_index.py unchanged.

### Approved delta (RP-1): canon governs the dev Reader start helper

Six binding canon passages, in five documents, require dev Reader start helpers to propagate APP\_ENV exactly as the caller supplies it, including unset and empty, and never to default it:

* HDE Architecture §3.8.1, "Repo-side start-helper posture";  
* HDE Mechanics Guide §5.6, "Dev Reader start helpers (APP\_ENV propagation)", and §34, the required start command LC\_ALL=C LANG=C TZ=UTC SAFE\_MODE=1 ALLOW\_NETWORK=0 APP\_ENV=dev PORT=8000 scripts/dev\_start\_reader.sh;  
* Glow Infrastructure §8.1, the APP\_ENV note;  
* HDE Build Checklist — Conjunction, HDE-CONJ001.4;  
* Glow QA Guide §13.9.

HDE Math Spec §12 supports them ("A default that treats an unset environment as development is not fail-closed").  
No HDE Build Notes addendum and no Product Owner rescope supersedes these passages, so canon governs the in-flight Plan, its review and the instruction on this point. For WU-PR-03:

1. scripts/dev\_start\_reader.sh is not changed; it already exports APP\_ENV only when the caller supplies it and displays \<UNSET\> otherwise. EC-4 is not implemented, and the script is not a deliverable of the unit.  
2. IF-10's last clause reads: "scripts/dev\_start\_reader.sh propagates APP\_ENV exactly as the caller supplies it, including unset and empty, and never defaults it; the canonical start command supplies APP\_ENV=dev."  
3. Acceptance (2)'s last clause reads: "scripts/dev\_start\_reader.sh with APP\_ENV=dev starts a serving runner; with APP\_ENV unset or empty it propagates that state and the runner refuses GET /reader (403 ERR\_READER\_FORBIDDEN)."  
4. Contract E-6 reads: "/reader served only for APP\_ENV=dev. The runner propagates APP\_ENV exactly and the caller supplies dev. VERIFY.sh uses the canonical runner and requires APP\_ENV=dev."  
5. The other instruction clauses that state the default follow items 1 to 4:  
   * in §6, "S10 gate and runner default" becomes "S10 gate and the start command with APP\_ENV=dev";  
   * "the runner-default test" becomes "the runner propagation test" in §6 and §10;  
   * §9 item 5 starts the runner with the caller's APP\_ENV=dev;  
   * §5.5's conclusion that the default is required no longer holds.  
6. VERIFY.sh supplies no default. Unless the caller supplies exactly APP\_ENV=dev, it refuses before starting a runner, prints VERIFY\_APP\_ENV\_NOT\_DEV with the effective value and exits 2\. Otherwise it passes APP\_ENV unchanged to the start helper.  
7. docs/server/reader\_v1.md and docs/acceptance/reader\_a7\_crib.md state the start command APP\_ENV=dev scripts/dev\_start\_reader.sh and that the script never defaults APP\_ENV.  
8. tests/scripts/test\_dev\_start\_reader.py stays the registered owner test of scripts/dev\_start\_reader.sh (the four classifier keys of the instruction's ID-3 are unchanged). It proves:  
   * APP\_ENV=dev serves;  
   * unset prints APP\_ENV=\<UNSET\> and refuses 403 ERR\_READER\_FORBIDDEN;  
   * empty, test, local and prod are kept and refused;  
   * VERIFY.sh with APP\_ENV=dev exits 0 with every token \_OK;  
   * VERIFY.sh without an explicit dev exits 2 with VERIFY\_APP\_ENV\_NOT\_DEV before any runner starts.  
9. It requires bash and curl.

### Approved delta (RP-3): one line leaves the current-release label roster

tools/evidence/update\_evidence\_index.py joins WU-PR-03's bounded files for exactly one change: the line "artifacts/core/purity/purity\_report.json", is removed from CURRENT\_RELEASE\_CAPTURE\_PATHS, leaving 22 paths.  
The engine-core purity report binds the SHA-256 of 12 engine-core, serializer, mechanics-catalog and schema sources. It carries no release\_id and does not bind catalog/manifest.json, so a same-version refresh that leaves those sources unchanged leaves it byte-stable. It therefore fails the roster's own stated criterion ("primaries whose bytes the admitted release determines"), and under the label rule it keeps a superseded release label.  
These do not change:

* any other line of the updater, the roster comment, the label rule and pattern, the label strip, CURRENT\_RELEASE\_LABEL\_MANIFEST\_INVALID and every other refusal, the write paths, the schema, the admitted-CRD table and the catalog validation call;  
* tools/evidence/generate\_engine\_core\_evidence.py and the purity report's schema.

The updater's own runs in the convergence remove the row's label; no governed artifact is hand-edited. For every other roster capture, a stale label after a refresh remains a finding to the Implementation Agent, never a roster edit.

### RP-2: in scope under the base; no delta

The updater validates the tracked endpoint catalog through the transport-proof owner's catalog validator. With WU-PR-03's validator, every updater run therefore refuses (aux narrative catalog invalid) until that owner has rewritten the catalog. The convergence runs the transport-proof owner's write and \--check after admission and before the canonical JSON gate and the first updater run (step 3b). The later run of that owner stays and is byte-stable. The restart point for an evidence-primary change is step 3b.  
This is a sequencing correction inside the method the approved Plan already sets: the same commands and owners, regeneration before the updater, and byte stability on a second run. No acceptance intent, boundary, unit or dependency changes.

### Binding requirements

* git diff from base to head is empty for scripts/dev\_start\_reader.sh. For tools/evidence/update\_evidence\_index.py it is exactly the one deleted roster line.  
* After the convergence's last updater run:  
  * the purity report's Index and Mirror rows carry no notes field;  
  * every other roster row carries the new release's label;  
  * the stale-binding sweep finds no purity-report hit.  
* The updater changes, so every test file that imports or runs it runs. The full tests/ sweep under closed rails, with ruby, bash and curl available, ends with a clean tree. The unit's result record names each check with its counts.  
* The unit's result record states the roster change, its reason and the one row it unlabels. It carries the nonclaim that the purity report is not a release-bound capture. It does not represent the change as a modification of WU-PR-02's accepted delivery.  
* WU-PR-03's acceptance (1) and (3) to (7), ID-1 to ID-4, the other contracts, the exclusions, the nonclaims, the evidence obligations and the PR-30/PR-35 split are unchanged. So are the /reader gate design (served only for explicit APP\_ENV=dev), the catalog env\_gate and acceptance (2)'s refusal set.  
* Any further change outside the bounded files as extended here is a new finding to the Implementation Agent through the rescope lane.

### Scope, exclusions and nonclaims

* No release member changes beyond the unit's own two (adapter/http\_reader.py, engine/cli/main.py); the same-version refresh is unchanged. scripts/dev\_start\_reader.sh, VERIFY.sh and the updater are not members.  
* No public route, public payload, public flag, transport or production behavior changes from this rescope.  
* No canon changes. The six start-helper passages stand as written, and no canon drainage follows from CR-6.  
* Keeping the start helper's default under a Product Owner decision superseding canon was not decided. It remains a Product Owner option, not recommended, and would require drainage of the six passages.  
* Adding release identity to the purity report was not approved.  
* No live vendor, production or database call; closed rails throughout.  
* No QA artifact, QA verdict, acceptance, PF09 or PF30 status movement, deployment, canon edit, Product Owner Proceed, merge authority or epic or CRD closure follows from this addendum.  
* WU-PR-01 and WU-PR-02 remain accepted-final; nothing in them is reopened or rerun.

### Affected surfaces

* Requirements. S10 and S09 are met within their approved intent: dev use supplies APP\_ENV=dev, as the canonical start command and the Dev and QA environment bindings do. S12 stays met; a capture the release does not determine carries no release claim. No other requirement is touched.  
* Dependencies. None upstream. WU-PR-04 and WU-PR-05 refresh against the 22-path roster. WU-PR-08's version bump changes an engine-core source, so it regenerates the purity report, which stays unlabelled.  
* Tests. The owner test proves propagation and the VERIFY.sh refusal instead of a default. No other test assertion changes. The updater's consumers run in the full sweep.  
* Ops and QA. The S10 Live QA step and the Ops task that places the QA candidate use the canonical start command with an explicit APP\_ENV. The unset refusal stays observable through the runner, which canon says QA harnesses rely on.  
* Documentation. Two repository documents state the start command with APP\_ENV=dev. AGENTS.md and README.md follow in the documentation unit.  
* Doc-delta candidates (canon, not edited). After S10, HDE Mechanics Guide §34's "Current repository posture" statement that the Reader handler treats missing APP\_ENV as dev becomes stale. This adds to the candidates already recorded for this unit.  
* Downstream work. The updater-consumer, label-timing and stale-label rules carried by addendum 2.5 apply to the 22-path roster. The step-3b order applies to any later unit that changes the catalog validator.

### Evidence

* Canon. On main 852370c, each of the six start-helper passages and the supporting HDE Math Spec bullet was read in its source; the quotations in the request are exact. On main, scripts/dev\_start\_reader.sh exports APP\_ENV only when it is set. VERIFY.sh line 4 is export APP\_ENV=\${APP\_ENV:-dev}.  
* Reproduction setup. A detached worktree of 852370c with the planning prototype applied, CPython 3.12 with the CI install, and closed rails.

* Convergence. The order with step 3b exited 0 at every step through the updater, orientation, path, Mirror-schema and hash checks.

### Conflicts

CR-1 to CR-5 of HDE-CRD-0002 are carried unchanged.

## 2.7 HDE-CRD-0002 WU-PR-03 corrective cycle: canon route postures govern the Endpoint Catalog, the dev Reader gate compares APP\_ENV exactly, and the canonical dev runner meets the required local capture profile

Timestamp: 101426 05:10 (UTC) Details: Records the architecture decisions for the corrective cycle of work unit WU-PR-03 of HDE-CRD-0002 (public, dev and CLI surfaces; requirements S02, S07, S09, S10, S15, S18). The unit's first pull request, [amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2\#643](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2/issues/643), merged as c98d41f with two open review findings.

* Origin: the Product Owner's direction of 2026-10-14 to re-plan the unit with its architecture decided in advance. The decisions were authored by the whole-change Implementation Agent.  
* Evidence: canon read on main at c98d41f, the served route set measured there, the PR-35 rescope request v1.0 and its prototype, and the governed env-gate proof.

### Decision and approval status

These six decisions govern the WU-PR-03 corrective plan and its implementation.

* They are made under the Product Owner's direction. They take effect for the corrective cycle once the Product Owner publishes them as Build Notes.  
* They resolve the two review findings that [\#643](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2/issues/643) merged with:  
  * five Endpoint Catalog rows publishing three canon-retired routes;  
  * a dev Reader gate that trims and case-folds APP\_ENV.  
* They settle in advance the remaining canon questions on the unit's surfaces, so that the corrective plan needs no further rescope for them.

The Specification is not edited. The immutable base is not rewritten; the clauses this addendum overlays are listed below. The canon-conflict register entries CR-7 and CR-8 are decided APPROVED, alternative (a) each, by this direction.

### Base and what landed

* What [\#643](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2/issues/643) landed on main:  
  * the governed not-found envelope (S02);  
  * the help text (S07);  
  * the dev harness retirement and the VERIFY.sh repoint (S09);  
  * the request-time /reader gate (S10);  
  * a 23-row Endpoint Catalog with the Aux record and its validator (S15);  
  * the document corrections (S18);  
  * the classifier registrations (ID-3) and the contract-inventory baseline (ID-4);  
  * the start-helper propagation and the 22-path label roster (Build Notes 2.6);  
  * a same-version release refresh with release\_id 72fca4c341d871dc75a828140ee1494714b322a13656bac93f66164060ea390f.  
* Corrective delta only. All of that stands except where a decision below changes it. The corrective cycle is a delta on main, not a re-implementation.

### Architecture decisions

AD-1 — Route postures govern Endpoint Catalog population.

* Accounting rule. Every (method, path) that adapter/factory.py, adapter/http\_reader.py::create\_app or adapter/wsgi.py serves is accounted for exactly once: either by one Endpoint Catalog record, or by one exclusion class with its basis. There are 53 distinct pairs at c98d41f.  
* The exclusion classes:  
  * (a) governed refusal;  
  * (b) alias of a canonical record;  
  * (c) framework-implicit method;  
  * (d) canon-retired route. HDE Architecture §3.5 "Current repository discrepancy" and HDE Users Guide §1.1 mark /ops/db/unavailable, /ops/probe/env and /ops/writer/diagnostic RETIRED/NONCANONICAL and exclude them from the Endpoint Catalog.  
* Class (d) holds nine pairs: GET, HEAD, OPTIONS of /ops/db/unavailable and of /ops/probe/env, and POST, HEAD, OPTIONS of /ops/writer/diagnostic.  
* Catalog changes. The five catalog records for those routes are removed. The catalog goes from 23 records to 18, and the exclusions from 30 to 35\. The catalog owner's validator refuses, fail-closed, any record whose path is canon-retired.  
* Unchanged:  
  * every other record, including /internal/healthz and /internal/readyz of adapter/wsgi.py, which no canon retires;  
  * the success\_endpoints designation (GET /reader);  
  * every route. No route is added, removed or changed.  
* S15 reading. Specification S15 ("Every registered route has a catalog entry, or the route is removed") is read under canon precedence. A registered route that canon explicitly retires and excludes from the Endpoint Catalog is accounted for by that exclusion, and its removal stays with its canon owner.

AD-2 — The dev Reader gate compares APP\_ENV exactly.

* Rule. GET /reader and its HEAD are served only when APP\_ENV is exactly the string dev. There is no trimming and no case-folding.  
* Refused. Unset, empty and every other value refuse with 403 ERR\_READER\_FORBIDDEN, as before. That includes dev, dev , Dev, DEV, test and local. Version validation stays first.  
* Basis. HDE-CLI-API-Vendor-Ref §5.4 "Gate" and §5.6 (/reader: "explicit APP\_ENV=dev"); HDE Schemas & Artifacts "Reader surface routes and distinct contracts (normative)"; HDE Mechanics Guide §34. Canon normalizes APP\_ENV only where normalization widens refusal (the compat production posture, HDE-CLI-API-Vendor-Ref §5.5). On this gate it would widen serving.  
* Unchanged: the catalog env\_gate (APP\_ENV=dev), VERIFY.sh's exact requirement and the start helper's propagation.

AD-3 — The canonical dev runner meets the required local capture profile. The runner is the dev harness that S09 made canonical.

* python \-m adapter.http\_reader binds 127.0.0.1 only. There is no host override, and port 8000 remains the default with an explicit PORT override.  
* It sets Flask debug=False, use\_debugger=False and use\_reloader=False explicitly.  
* It emits no CORS response header. No Access-Control-\* header appears on any response, including a cross-origin preflight OPTIONS. A test proves this, because canon requires a test-backed disabled-CORS declaration.  
* The start helper's banner names the actual host. One line of scripts/dev\_start\_reader.sh changes; its APP\_ENV propagation is unchanged.  
* Basis: HDE-CLI-API-Vendor-Ref §5.4 "Gate" ("bound only to a local interface (for example, 127.0.0.1)") and HDE Mechanics Guide §34 "Required local capture profile".  
* Shared environments. A shared environment that needs another bind uses an infrastructure-provided start command (HDE Mechanics Guide §34 "Shared environments"), not this runner.

AD-4 — Production exposure of non-public routes is decided once, in the HDE-CRD-0002 security review, not in WU-PR-03. No WU-PR-03 requirement covers these four canon gaps, and fixing them changes the production behavior of routes outside S02 to S18:

1. The production factories mount dev-harness routes:  
   * the routes: GET/HEAD /reader, POST /internal/dev/sampler and the three /dev/\*/conjunction routes;  
   * the canon: HDE Architecture §3.2 "Non-prod entries must be unreachable in production" and §3.3 "Harness is dev-only; never mounted in production", with its "Current repository discrepancy"; HDE-CLI-API-Vendor-Ref §5.4 "must not be mounted in production builds or non-dev deploys";  
   * the unprefixed POST /reader refusal stub shares this path.  
2. The three canon-retired /ops routes stay mounted and ungated in the production factory. /ops/probe/env returns process identity and start time.  
3. The dev/admin gate used by the sampler and conjunction routes trims and case-folds, although HDE Governance "Dev/admin APP\_ENV gating" allows "exactly" dev, test and local.  
4. adapter/http\_reader.py::create\_app's after-request hook never fires.

Why they are decided there:

* A correct fix is one factory-level design applied uniformly to every non-public route. Requirement S19 (the HDE-CRD-0002 code security review, work unit WU-PR-05) is the only HDE-CRD-0002 requirement whose scope reaches their exposure. S19 fixes each confirmed finding within HDE-CRD-0002 or routes it to an owner with a reason.  
* Until then, the S10 control for /reader is the request-time refusal. That matches the governed env-gate proof, which records /reader\_success\_unreachable=true under APP\_ENV=prod.

AD-5 — One corrective release refresh.

* Trigger. AD-2 and AD-3 change the release member adapter/http\_reader.py. The unit therefore cuts a same-version manifest at version 1.3.0 and built time 2026-08-24T18:04:49Z, then converges the governed evidence through the owners.  
* Order:  
  * the catalog owner runs first after admission (step 3b), because its validator changes (AD-1);  
  * every owner and validator is byte-stable on a second pass;  
  * a member byte change after the cut restarts at the cut;  
  * an evidence-primary change restarts at step 3b.  
* New base. The new release\_id becomes the refresh base for WU-PR-04 and WU-PR-05, and 72fca4c3… becomes historical.  
* Unchanged: the 22-path label roster, the evidence-index updater and every frozen family.

AD-6 — Canon trace is part of the deliverable.

* Plan and record. The corrective plan and the unit's result record map each decision, and each route the unit touches, to the canon document, section and bullet relied on.  
* Enumeration test. The route-enumeration test records every excluded pair's class and basis. A served pair with no recorded posture, or a record for a canon-retired path, fails continuous integration.

### Overlaid base clauses

For WU-PR-03 only. Every other word of each clause holds.

| Base clause | Becomes |
| :---- | :---- |
| Implementation Plan v1.1 §5 IF-6: the internal rows for /ops/db/unavailable, /ops/probe/env and /ops/writer/diagnostic | withdrawn (AD-1) |
| Audit EC-8 (instruction §4): "Catalog every registered route; remove none" | "Catalog every registered route that canon does not retire; remove none" |
| Instruction ID-1 binding reading and its exclusion classes (a) to (c) | classes (a) to (d) as AD-1 states |
| Instruction §9.2: "Normalize the value as \_dev\_admin\_gate does (strip, lower), but serve only dev" | "Compare the raw value exactly; serve only dev" (AD-2) |
| Instruction §7: "No change to the 0.0.0.0 bind or to the production mount of the gated dev blueprint" | the bind is changed by AD-3; the production mount stays excluded (AD-4) |
| Build Notes 2.6 approved delta item 1 and its binding requirement that scripts/dev\_start\_reader.sh has an empty diff | the script changes in its banner's host text only (AD-3); APP\_ENV propagation is unchanged |
| WU-PR-03 detailed plan v1.1 §6.2 and PD-5 (normalization), and §6.5 records 5 and 8 to 11 | superseded by AD-2 and AD-1 |

### Binding requirements

* Catalog. The catalog has 18 records with none for a canon-retired path. The validator refuses a canon-retired record. The enumeration test accounts for all served pairs: 18 catalogued plus 35 excluded equals 53 at c98d41f, or the then-current served count, with the arithmetic stated.  
* Exact gate:  
  * the served test admits exactly dev;  
  * the refusal matrix includes unset, empty, dev, dev , Dev, DEV, test, local, prod, production, live and an arbitrary value;  
  * docs/server/reader\_v1.md states "exactly dev".  
* Runner profile:  
  * the runner's run call carries host="127.0.0.1", debug=False, use\_debugger=False and use\_reloader=False;  
  * a test proves no Access-Control-\* header on a dev-runner GET /reader, an OPTIONS preflight with Origin and Access-Control-Request-Method, and an error response;  
  * scripts/dev\_start\_reader.sh and docs/server/reader\_v1.md name host 127.0.0.1;  
  * the start-helper owner test and VERIFY.sh pass on the loopback bind.  
* Evidence and checks:  
  * the governed catalog family, the transport proofs and every evidence family the refresh regenerates are produced by their owners only;  
  * frozen families keep their bytes;  
  * the contract-inventory baseline still equals the discovered records;  
  * the architecture snapshot test stays green;  
  * the full tests/ sweep passes under closed rails;  
  * the two convergence passes are byte-identical;  
  * the release attestation builds and verifies.  
* No skips. No test is skipped, deselected, marked expected-failure or quarantined.

### Scope, exclusions and nonclaims

* Changed: catalog content (AD-1), one gate comparison (AD-2), the dev runner's run call and the start helper's banner (AD-3), two documents, tests, and the release refresh (AD-5).  
* Not changed: no route added, removed, renamed or re-gated other than the /reader comparison. No public contract, payload, flag or transport change. No production factory change.  
* Out of WU-PR-03: AD-4's four gaps.  
* Not edited: no Specification edit, no permanent-canon edit, no admission-constant or version change.  
* Not established: no QA verdict, acceptance, Ops, deployment, PF09 or PF30 status movement or closure follows from this addendum. [\#643](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2/issues/643)'s merge and this addendum do not establish acceptance of WU-PR-03.

### Affected surfaces

* Requirements. S10 (exact gate; local bind) and S15 (canon-read population) are corrected. S09's canonical runner meets the harness profile. S02, S07 and S18 are unchanged.  
* Tests.  
  * The enumeration test gains class (d), the retired-path pin and retargeted validator cases.  
  * The transport gate test changes its served and refused values.  
  * A CORS-absence test and runner-profile assertions are added.  
* Evidence. The catalog family, its sidecars and path proofs, and every family the same-version refresh regenerates, with the Index and Mirror through the updater.  
* Ops and QA. The S10 Live QA step proves the request-time refusal in the deployed target, as before. S15 stays a catalog-entries-only change. The dev runner is reachable only on loopback.  
* Downstream:  
  * WU-PR-04 and WU-PR-05 refresh from the new release\_id;  
  * WU-PR-05's S19 review receives AD-4's four gaps as named inputs;  
  * WU-PR-08's port and bind statements (S17) describe the loopback bind;  
  * the repository documentation unit states the start command and bind;  
  * a later unit that changes the catalog validator runs its owner at step 3b.  
* Canon doc-delta candidates (not edited):  
  * HDE Schemas & Artifacts §8.8: the population rule, including canon-retired exclusion, public\_aux and aliases;  
  * HDE-CLI-API-Vendor-Ref §5.6 "Implementation and acceptance state";  
  * HDE Mechanics Guide §34 "Current repository posture" (bind, flags, CORS, APP\_ENV default), and its route table's /api/reader alias row;  
  * HDE Architecture §3.4, which labels the health probes speculative, while HDE Mechanics Guide §32.3 requires them.

### Evidence

* Served set at c98d41f: 53 distinct pairs across the three factories. The catalog holds 23 records, five of them for the three canon-retired routes. The canon-retired pairs number nine.  
* Dev runner and gate at c98d41f:  
  * adapter/http\_reader.py runs create\_app().run(host="0.0.0.0", port=…) with no debugger or reloader arguments;  
  * no module under adapter/ or engine/http/ emits a CORS header or imports a CORS extension;  
  * the gate returns raw.strip().lower() \== "dev";  
  * the start helper's banner prints host=0.0.0.0.  
* Env-gate proof. The governed env-gate proof records APP\_ENV=prod with /reader\_success\_unreachable=true.  
* PR-35 prototype. It removed the five records, added class (d) and the retired pin, and regenerated the catalog family: validators exit 0, 253 tests passed. It also implemented the exact comparison with a re-cut: 28 tests passed. Its release\_id value is not binding.

### Conflicts

* CR-7 (CANON\_RECONCILIATION): Specification S15 and the approved Plan's IF-6 and EC-8 catalogued three routes that HDE Architecture §3.5 excludes. Decided APPROVED, alternative (a): exclusion under canon, as AD-1 states.  
* CR-8 (CANON\_RECONCILIATION): the instruction's normalizing gate conflicted with the explicit APP\_ENV=dev passages. Decided APPROVED, alternative (a): exact comparison, as AD-2 states.

## 2.11 Every requirement and work unit carries a business case; work rests on real inputs only

Timestamp: 102026 10:35 (UTC). Details: Establishes that no requirement or work unit is done without a stated business case or on inputs the product never has, and sets the rules for where the business case is checked, what counts as a real input, the ban on emulating external systems, how review findings are dispositioned, and when a repeated finding class stops the fixing of instances. Origin: the HDE-CRD-0002 WU-PR-06 postmortem v1.0 (HDE-CRD-0002-WU-PR-06-postmortem-v1.0.md, INCIDENT\_RCA, analysis state COMPLETE, author session https\://claude.ai/code/session\_01NZEtMVb5HR3XYEmJ6W5cb6), written at the Product Owner's request in the WU-PR-06 PR-20/PR-30 session before PR-40. Evidence: the postmortem's reading of the Specification, Implementation Plan v1.1, the PR instruction and plan, results v1.0 to v1.24, the GitHub Actions run list and the pull request record of [amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2\#660](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2/issues/660).

### Source and authority

This addendum records the Product Owner's rules, in the Product Owner's words: "Every PR must have a business case. It cannot simply be for the sake of evidence or CI." and "no work is ever done based on inputs the product will never have." The Product Owner directed that entry into HDE Build Notes is adoption (direction of 2026-10-20 in the authoring conversation). Where this addendum conflicts with earlier PF-Canon or with an in-flight change document, this addendum governs for the scope below.

### Rule

1. Business case. Every Specification requirement (Epic or CRD) and every work unit states:  
   * its beneficiary: who gains (Glow users, the production operator, or the delivery team);  
   * its evidence: the observed incidents or measurements that show the problem, cited; a forecast is not evidence;  
   * its benefit: what changes for the beneficiary, in user outcome, risk, time or money;  
   * its measure: how the benefit is checked after delivery;  
   * its cost ceiling: the most the work may spend before it returns to the Product Owner, in sessions and elapsed days. This is a business-case budget and not a CI-budget control.  
2. More complete or more faithful evidence, CI, tooling or records for their own sake, conformance to canon, and "could prevent" without an observed incident are not a business case. Internal tooling qualifies only when its expected saving, from observed incident data, exceeds its ceiling.  
3. Gates.  
   * Specification approval blocks a requirement without a business case, for CRDs and Epics alike.  
   * IA-30 checks each work unit's business case and ceiling.  
   * PR-10 carries both into the instruction, and PR-20 states how the plan stays within the ceiling.  
   * The Proceed is given against them.  
   * A unit whose business case is met stops; further work on it needs a new business case.  
   * A unit that passes its ceiling has a change of "budget, schedule or risk needing Product Owner direction", which is material under the existing definition, and the work returns to the Product Owner before it continues.  
4. Real inputs. Requirements, designs, tests, fixes and review dispositions rest on real inputs. An input is real when it:  
   * (a) exists on main, or in an approved unit's planned deliverable;  
   * (b) occurs in the product's real operating environment: the deployed services, the hosted CI runner, the operator's shell, the vendor's responses; or  
   * (c) can be supplied by a user, a vendor or an attacker through an interface the product exposes.  
5. An input constructed only to exercise code justifies no requirement, design element, test, fix or blocking finding. An input outside the real set gets one refusal or a stated limit, never a model of it.  
6. No emulation of external systems. A tool or test does not reimplement the behavior of an external system: GitHub Actions, pip and Python packaging, YAML, a vendor API, PostgreSQL. It pins the exact input it supports and refuses any other, or it calls the real system.  
7. Finding disposition.  
   * A review finding, from Codex or from a person, is fixed when it shows a wrong result, a crash or a guarantee breach on a real input (rule 3).  
   * Otherwise the reply on the thread gives the traced path and the stated limit, and the thread is resolved.  
   * Declining a P1 or a security finding goes to the Product Owner first.  
   * A blanket instruction to fix every finding on a PR is read under this rule.  
   * This makes the dispositions PO-35-5 and PO-35-6 of the WU-PR-06 pull request the standing default: fix only a wrong result on a real input, or a breach of the read-only or no-secrets guarantees; a snapshot-precision finding is a hypothetical-input finding unless it shows a write that the tool itself, or code it runs in the checkout, can make.  
8. Repeated finding class. When two consecutive review rounds on one PR raise findings of the same class (the same component or contract), the session stops fixing instances and asks the Product Owner one design question: narrow the design, accept a stated limit, or rescope. This is a design trigger by finding class. It is not a push count, a pause threshold or a CI-budget control, and it leaves the Change Process Guide §0.2 rule "There is no five-update circuit breaker" unchanged.

### Scope and supersession

The rules apply from this addendum to every Epic and CRD, including units of in-flight changes not yet at PR-10. They extend the Epic Business Case rules (HDE Governance §9.7.7, "Business Case section required (Epic Specifications)", and Plan Templates "Business Case (MUST)") to CRD requirements and to work units, where canon asked only for a "Warrant" for the whole change (HDE CRD Records §4.1 "Initial registration": "Observed need", "Warrant", "Lead Developer finding"). They add a PR-review trigger beside the Live QA churn rules (Change Process Guide §1.1.4; Plan Templates "QoS stop-rule: iteration churn escalation"), which cover Live QA only. They do not change the CI-budget rules of Change Process Guide §0.2 or Plan Templates "CI lane classification and automated budget-control discipline". The rules do not weaken the existing duty to fix a real defect; the real security finding of the postmortem (CX6-15, P1) stays a fix under rule 5\.

### Findings behind the rules (WU-PR-06, requirement S20)

* Outcome. WU-PR-06 spent 24 Codex review rounds, 51 findings, 24 completed hosted CI runs (7.5 hours of runner time) and 21 hours of PR-35 work, and the business case for it was never established. S20's stated purpose was fewer failed paid CI runs; its only evidence was PR04's ([\#467](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2/issues/467)) 4 failed runs out of 19\. Those 4 were catchable by running CI's own steps locally (the changed-test step with 4 tests, an owner-guard tuple that omitted a new consumer, a test that wrote a timestamp into a tracked file and failed the clean-tree check, and one rails-lane failure), and \#467's session reproduced all seven lanes directly under closed rails. The business function was delivered at PR-30: the replay ran all 16 steps of the real ci.yml and passed (CI\_APPLICABILITY\_AND\_EXACT\_HEAD\_OK), and hosted CI passed on the same head (d2ed4fc). 49 of the 51 findings needed a ci.yml, pyproject.toml, requirements-dev.txt, environment or Git state that no commit on main has ever had and no planned unit creates; the fixes for 41 of them were later deleted, or left unable to change any outcome, by the redesign PO-35-4.  
* Three causes. (1) Canon requires a Business Case only for Epics; a CRD requirement needs only a warrant, S20 had none in the approved Specification, and nothing measured it. (2) The design promised fidelity to external systems over open-ended inputs (replay whatever ci.yml a candidate has; fail closed on any construct not understood), made by PR plan v1.0 (PD-8), so every constructed input became a real defect against the plan. (3) Nothing told the reviewer, the PR-35 prompt or the round-7 decision to ask whether an input is real, and canon has no churn trigger for PR review; the loop stopped only when the Product Owner imposed the real-input test (PO-35-5, PO-35-6).  
* Cost. Codex review rounds 24; findings 51 (50 P2 and 1 P1; 47 fixed, 1 resolved by the pin (CX6-47), 3 declined as stated limits (CX6-12, CX6-49, CX6-51)); hosted CI runs on [\#660](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2/issues/660) 25 (23 success, 1 failure on 7a3b7b2 from an intermittent close-pack test, 1 in progress), completed-run wall time 451.6 minutes; PR-35 elapsed 21 h 16 min (13:00Z 19 Oct to 10:16Z 20 Oct); 50 commits; 25 result records (v1.0 to v1.24); tools/ci/prepush\_check.py 1,229 lines at PR-30, 2,132 at the pushed peak, 1,755 at head; owner test 552 lines and 21 cases at PR-30, 1,229 lines and 141 cases at the peak, 1,070 lines and 137 cases at head; PR diff \+6,789 lines in 28 files. Codex findings on the final pull request of sibling units of the same CRD and reviewer: WU-PR-01 ([\#630](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2/issues/630)) 0; WU-PR-02 ([\#637](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2/issues/637)) 0; WU-PR-04 ([\#651](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2/issues/651)) 1 (P1); WU-PR-05 ([\#656](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2/issues/656)) 1 (P2); WU-PR-06 ([\#660](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2/issues/660)) 51 (addenda 2.4, 2.5, 2.9 and 2.10); the volume came from what WU-PR-06 built and not from the reviewer.  
* Business case, tested. Beneficiary: PR sessions and the Product Owner. Evidence: one incident ([\#467](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2/issues/467)), with the briefs listing \#467, [\#492](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2/issues/492), [\#501](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2/issues/501) and [\#508](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2/issues/508) as intake evidence and no count of avoidable failures recorded. Benefit: fewer failed hosted runs, never quantified. Measure: none defined. Ceiling: none set. The problem was catchable and the function was delivered at PR-30; the unit then cost 24 completed hosted runs, six times the 4 failed runs that were its whole evidence, plus 21 hours of PR-35 work. S20 was a reasonable hypothesis with one data point that became a requirement without a beneficiary measure or a cost ceiling, and once work began nothing asked whether the next round of fixes bought anything.  
* The 51 findings by class (defined by where the triggering input would have to come from):

| Class | Subject | Count | Real input? |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| A. Python packaging | pip resolution, PEP 440/508/621, extras, build backends, editable metadata, install-step text (CX6-1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7, 8, 11, 14, 23, 27, 28, 30, 34, 35\) | 15 | No, except CX6-1 (the caller's versions can drift from CI's, though hosted CI still decides). pyproject.toml and requirements-dev.txt have one commit each on main (their creation, 2026-08-06); requirements-dev.txt has never had an extra and build-backend has never changed |
| B. GitHub Actions and YAML | runner labels, action revisions, step conditions, loose equality, env shapes, duplicate keys, tabs, block and flow scalars, job sets, output files (CX6-6, 9, 10, 12, 13, 18, 20, 21, 22, 25, 26, 29, 31, 32, 33, 36, 37, 38, 39, 40, 41, 42, 44, 45, 47, 49\) | 26 | No. Each needs a ci.yml edit no commit has made: across its 10 commits on main, runs-on was always ubuntu-latest, no tab ever appeared, the actions were always checkout@v4 and setup-python@v5, and persist-credentials has been false since added (CX6-25's second job existed before the HDE-EPIC039 consolidation to the one test job). No planned unit edits ci.yml |
| C. Self-check precision | the before-and-after snapshot missing a write that nothing makes (CX6-16, 19, 24, 43, 46, 48, 50, 51\) | 8 | No observed path; each was confirmed only by constructing its condition (a write into .git or ignored files in a scratch repository, or a TMPDIR inside the checkout). After PO-35-4 no repository code runs in the caller's checkout (PO-35-6) |
| D. Real | caller secrets reached child processes (P1, CX6-15); the sweep reported PASS without its tools when no test was uncovered (CX6-17) | 2 | Yes |

* What became of the fixes. PO-35-4 returned the install check to presence only (plan §5.7's original rule for the install step, before IF-35-7, IF-35-12 and IF-35-14 added the modelling; result v1.22, IF-35-23), so the class A modelling was deleted; the pin refuses any ci.yml other than the pinned one before parsing it, so the class B fixes can no longer change an outcome. Codex was measuring the code against the plan's own promise, for example CX6-42: "omitting them from this guard violates the replay parser's fail-closed contract".  
* Timeline decisions. Round 7: PO-35-1 declines CX6-12; PO-35-2, asked "What rule should I apply to further findings of that kind?" with "Fix if real and small (recommended)", answers "Fix every finding". Round 8: CX6-15 (the one P1) fixed under PO-35-3, an allowlisted child environment; CX6-16 and CX6-17 fixed. Rounds 9 to 21: CX6-18 to CX6-45 each fixed under PO-35-2, the tool growing to a pushed peak of 2,132 lines. 20 Oct 07:22 after round 22 (CX6-46 to CX6-48): PR-35 escalates; PO-35-4 pins ci.yml by SHA-256, reduces the install check to presence only and runs the classifier only in copies; PO-35-5 replaces PO-35-2 (fix only a wrong result on "the repository's real ci.yml, real install step or real tree", or a breach of the read-only or no-secrets guarantees; CX6-46 and CX6-48 fixed, CX6-47 resolved by the pin). Round 23: CX6-49 declined, CX6-50 fixed. 20 Oct 10:08: PO-35-6 ("A snapshot-precision finding is a hypothetical-input finding unless it shows a write that the tool itself or code it runs in the checkout can make"); CX6-51 declined. 20 Oct 10:16: MERGE\_PENDING (result v1.24, bd2dc3a); hosted CI run 39406716584 on bd2dc3a was in progress at writing.  
* Causal chain and the gaps the rules close.

| Stage | Gap | Owner of the gap |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| Intake and Specification | S20 entered with a forecast for a warrant, no measure and no ceiling; the Lead Developer finding says "each requirement below is an observed defect or gap with a cited source" while S20's source was a convenience need | Canon: HDE Governance §9.7.7 and Plan Templates "Business Case (MUST)" apply to Epics only; HDE CRD Records asks only for "Warrant" |
| Specification review | The duplication risk ("S20 duplicates CI logic and must track ci.yml changes") was named, not bounded | The CRD Specification approval route |
| Implementation Plan and IA-30 | S20 was allocated to WU-PR-06 with no budget | IA-30 |
| PR-10, ID-8 | The bar "runs what CI would run", reading the head's ci.yml with no YAML dependency, made the tool a GitHub Actions emulator over any candidate workflow | PR-10 has no rule to pin what you depend on |
| PR-20, plan v1.0 (PD-8) | The plan promised the parser would fail closed on any construct it did not understand and gave excluded steps substituted outcomes, turning an open-ended input language into a contract to defend; a pin or a stated precondition was available at planning time (the plan's author records this one as the author's own) | PR-20 has no real-input-set rule |
| PR-30 | The function was delivered and working | No check exists that a met business case stops further work |
| PR-35, rounds 1 to 6 | Each P2 was classified a "substantive in-scope correction" and fixed; the PR-35 prompt's only decline classes are "already resolved, duplicate/superseded, unsupported/out of scope, evidence pending, or material change" | The PR-35 prompt, step 3 |
| PR-35, round 7 | A blanket "Fix every finding" was chosen over the recommended "Fix if real and small" | No standing disposition rule |
| PR-35, rounds 8 to 22 | Same-class findings recurred for 15 more rounds; the instruction's materiality test includes "budget, schedule or risk needing Product Owner direction" but no budget existed, so it never tripped | Canon's churn rules (Change Process Guide §1.1.4; Plan Templates "QoS stop-rule: iteration churn escalation") cover Live QA only |
| Codex code review | Constructed-input findings were reported as P2 | AGENTS.md "Code review guidelines" excludes Markdown and CI-exempt paths only |

* Effect on WU-PR-06 had the rules been in force. Rule 1 would have stopped S20 at the Specification or given it a ceiling; rule 4 would have led the PR-20 plan to the pin it eventually adopted; rule 6 would have tripped at round 2, when CX6-2 and CX6-3 repeated CX6-1's class.  
* What went right. PR-30 delivered a working tool in one session; the one real security finding (CX6-15, P1) was caught and fixed; 23 of 24 completed CI runs passed (the cost was revalidating each fix, not failures); PR-35 escalated with options and PO-35-4, PO-35-5 and PO-35-6 ended the loop within three rounds and shrank the tool by 377 lines from its pushed peak.

### Epic, task, and status effects

* The rules apply immediately to the remaining HDE-CRD-0002 units. The postmortem's screen of them under rules 1 and 3 found: WU-PR-07 (S21: close-pack writer for Epic and CRD identities, close-run evidence family, ADR draft) has as its only evidence one incident (HDE-EPIC040 needed exceptional closure), states no benefit in product terms and has no measure or ceiling, so it needs a rule 1 business case before its PR-10, weighed against cheaper options such as a canon change to the close-report requirement or keeping exceptional closure; WU-PR-08 (S01 every release member in the wheel; S16 GEO\_API\_KEY in .env.example; S17 docs/ADAPTER\_009.md corrections; the release 1.4.0 cut) is open for S01 on rule 3, because whether production installs from a wheel is unknown and the Railway build command and deploy trigger are a PF07 gap that WU-OPS-01 discovers only after WU-PR-08, so that fact is established before WU-PR-08's PR-10, while S16, S17 and the cut are real; WU-DOC-01 (final repository documentation) has real operators and agents as beneficiaries and shrinks with any scope dropped; WU-OPS-01 (place the QA candidate in production; record the deployment facts) carries the strongest business case left (S02, a live public production defect, and S10, a fail-open security default, reach users only when deployed), and whether WU-PR-03's merged fixes for S02 and S10 are already live depends on the same unknown deploy trigger.  
* Work unit WU-PR-06 (S20): the pull request [\#660](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-hdengine-v2/pull/660) was open at MERGE\_PENDING (result v1.24, head bd2dc3a) with PR-40 waiting on it. Its disposition is the Product Owner's decision and is not made by this addendum.
